# Telegram Stock-Mention Watch

> State file for [Routine 6 — Telegram Stock-Mention Scan](../../framework/automation-schedule.md#routine-6--telegram-stock-mention-scan) (`/telegram-scan`). Overwritten in place each run — git history preserves prior state, same convention as [ibkr.md](ibkr.md) and [ibkr-orders.md](ibkr-orders.md). Tracks the last-seen post per monitored channel (so no post is reprocessed or silently skipped) plus a running log of every company mention the command has evaluated, whether or not it triggered an action.

Post text never used as a financial input per Rule 0 (only the reason the ticker was looked at) — live price independently refetched via IBKR: **$163.00** (contract_id 233674866, NASDAQ), **+21.97% on the day**, confirming the post's "premarket is looking good" framing is directionally real, though CHTR remains 61.4% below its 52-week high ($422.29). yfinance failed identically to the AAPL/WSE precedents (`curl_cffi` TLS SSLError, same diagnosed environment-level incompatibility) — fallback per Rule 0's documented contingency was CHTR's own audited SEC Form 10-K (FY2025, accession 0001091667-26-000017) and Form 10-Q (Q1 2026, accession 0001091667-26-000028, read for Cox/Liberty Broadband deal status only). **Phase 01 gate FAILS on four independent criteria** under both threshold sets this framework carries: net margin (9.10% FY25 best year, never clears >15%/>12% in any of 3 years), ROIC (8.58% vs >15%), revenue growth (3yr CAGR 0.46%, essentially flat — revenue actually declined -0.6% FY25 vs FY24), and Net Debt/EBITDA (4.15× company-disclosed / 4.22× independently recomputed, vs <2×/<2.5× — no Hybrid Upgrade 5 asset-light-financial exception applies to a capital-intensive cable operator, and CHTR's own disclosed target leverage policy of 4.0–4.5× pre-Cox-close confirms this is a deliberate capital-structure choice, not a one-off spike); FCF/NI conversion also mixed-fails the literal "2+ consecutive years >70%" reading. Passes: gross margin proxy (~40.2%, borderline/expanding), FCF positive all 5 years shown, FCF yield (21.40–24.24%, well above >4% — the one metric where the post-merger-news price pop still looks statistically cheap), EV/EBIT (9.02× vs <20×), no dilutive issuance (net repurchaser, -17.0% share count FY22→FY25). The move itself is independently corroborated as M&A-driven, not fundamentals-driven: CHTR is mid-pending on a $34.5B Cox Communications merger / Liberty Broadband combination (FCC-approved, expected mid-2026 close), confirmed in CHTR's own Q1 2026 10-Q — this context was read for corroboration only, never used as a financial input. Per `new-position.md` step 2, the session stopped at the failed gate — no Phase 02 score, no Rate Gate, no DCF, no order setup. No position recommended. Next review trigger set on the Cox/Liberty Broadband deal closing, a sustained revenue-decline reversal, a credible deleveraging path, or a management change. Priority tier: **P2** (0% portfolio weight, not held; not a Rule-9-style RESCORE on a ≥5% holding and no trim/exit trigger fired) — no `.ics` sent (outcome is PASS — do not enter, not BUY/TRIM/EXIT, same precedent as WSE 2026-06-26). Post text never used as data per Rule 0 — live price independently re-pulled fresh via IBKR (`get_price_snapshot`, contract_id 265598): **$274.22, -6.44% on the day** (prior close implied $293.08), 52-week range $198.65–$317.40 — confirms a real, large, same-day decline consistent with the post's Nasdaq-reaction claim, independent of the post's own framing. **Evaluation could not proceed past Rule 0's live-data step**: yfinance (`Ticker.info`, `.history()`, and every other accessor) failed identically with `curl_cffi.requests.exceptions.SSLError: ... TLS connect error: ... OPENSSL_internal: invalid library`. Diagnosed as an environment-level incompatibility, not a transient fault: yfinance's underlying `curl_cffi` library performs browser-TLS-fingerprint impersonation via its own bundled TLS stack, which this session's policy-enforcing proxy (see `/root/.ccr/README.md`) cannot re-terminate — the README's own unsupported-class list names exactly this ("certificate-pinned clients... If a tool still cannot work through the proxy, report it"). Confirmed it isn't a proxy-config gap: `CURL_CA_BUNDLE`/`SSL_CERT_FILE`/`REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE` are all already correctly set to the proxy's CA bundle, and plain `curl`/OpenSSL reach Yahoo's endpoints fine (got a normal HTTP 429 rate-limit, not a TLS error). Tried a plain-`requests` fallback (bypassing `curl_cffi` entirely) directly against Yahoo's `quoteSummary` API: got HTTP 401 "Invalid Crumb" — Yahoo's own bot-detection now requires the cookie/crumb handshake that only the blocked `curl_cffi` TLS-impersonation path can complete, so this isn't fixable by routing around `curl_cffi`. Checked the Interactive Brokers MCP toolset for an alternative fundamentals source (income statement / balance sheet / cash flow / margins / ROIC): none exists — only price/options/themes/connections/account tools are exposed. Per CLAUDE.md Rule 0 ("never invent or estimate financial data — if a metric is missing, stop and ask") and this command's explicit unattended contingency, **the auto-commit for AAPL is skipped**: no Phase 01 gate, no score, no `sessions/` file, and no watchlist entry were created or committed for AAPL this run, since none of those criteria are obtainable from either approved data source in this environment. Flagged in GitHub issue for manual follow-up once a working fundamentals source is available (or the proxy/`curl_cffi` incompatibility is fixed) — `/new-position AAPL` should be re-run manually at that point; this is a genuine, identified gap, not a "no action" mention. |
